# Repentance: fresh decisions on every October 11-12 change

## Result and scope

The maintainer requested a new decision for **every** previous change, not a
blanket rollback. This review covers all **397 records** in the original plan:
**367 reader-wording revisions** and **30 decisions to retain contextual wording**.
Every record also receives an updated explanation and audit trail. These are
records, not unique Bible verses: chapter records and per-unit versions overlap.
The four Jeremiah/Luke records from the October 12 follow-up are included.

**Decision:** restore repent/repentance for moral-spiritual change where suitable,
and explain it rather than replace it with a technical word. Keep regret,
reconsideration, change of mind and turning expressions where their context
requires them. Do not recreate the older inconsistent cognitive-only renderings.
Do not erase genuine sorrow, or equate repentance with sorrow or its resulting
works. Familiarity supports readability, not an assertion of lexical accuracy.
Actual reader reception has not been measured.

### Important fresh judgments

- Mark 1:15: **Repent and believe**; the explanation belongs in the note.
- 2 Corinthians 7:9-10: **repentance**; keep sorrow as its cause, not its synonym.
- Acts 20:21: **repentance toward God**, because the destination is explicit.
- Acts 3:19 and 26:20: **repent** plus the separate turn/return expression.
- Luke 17:3-4: **if he repents / I repent**, in both editions. Do not make the
  offender prove completed reform before receiving the commanded forgiveness.
- Jeremiah 31:19: **keep** the latest sorrow wording and the turning alternative.
- Hebrews 12:17 and Job 42:6: **keep** the contextual renderings with ambiguity
  disclosed; this terminology review does not settle their wider interpretation.
- 2 Clement 9: correct **away from a sincere heart** to **from a sincere heart**.
  The recorded English witness says "Repentance from a sincere heart." The old
  replacement had reversed the function of *from*.
- Hermas 15: distinguish **repent** in section 2 from **change their mind** in
  section 3, where people abandon baptism. Align the chapter and unit records.
- Gad 5:6: **changed my attitude toward Joseph**; align POB/Simplified and chapter/
  unit representations. Judah 15:4: **repented of these things**, consistent with
  recorded metanoesas, its conduct context, and the corresponding 8:25 passage.
- Keep the earlier **more joy ... than** correction in Luke 15:7 and all existing
  source-backed emphasis. Fix malformed live lexical metadata such as
  "reorientationd mind"; never edit archived reasoning to hide the earlier error.

### Evidence and limitations

This is an AI-assisted editorial reassessment of each record's existing source
or English witness, surrounding reader wording, prior decision and revised
wording. It is **not independent scholarly review**, full manuscript collation,
or fresh ancient-language verification of English-witness adaptations. For
Simplified POB, the paired POB source and decision remain controlling. All records
remain drafts needing review. Deterministic tests establish scope, provenance,
application and export consistency, not theological consensus or semantic perfection.

Lexical resources freshly consulted during this review:
[Mounce, metanoia](https://www.billmounce.com/greek-dictionary/metanoia),
[Mounce, metamelomai](https://www.billmounce.com/greek-dictionary/metamelomai),
[BDB, nacham](https://biblehub.com/hebrew/5162.htm), and
[NET, Hebrews 12:17](https://classic.net.bible.org/verse.php?book=heb&chapter=12&verse=17).
The recorded source for each passage is identified in its YAML and pinned by
source hash in the new plan. The lexicons support contextual ranges, not approval
of every record or of a new mandatory English term.

The original October 11 plan and October 12 follow-up are retained unchanged.
All 397 input files are pinned to baseline `1c388ebf7b81430b6dbf56575de3b3604ba77804`.
The exact current output, per-record reason, input/source hashes, source-evidence
limitations and lexical updates are in
[`config/terminology/repentance-contextual-2026-10-13.json`](../config/terminology/repentance-contextual-2026-10-13.json).
The applicator preflights all records before any write and is idempotent.
Sources, original generation data, frozen editions, other languages and earlier
revision history are not rewritten. Old policy tests replay the frozen input;
a separate complete suite tests the active contract. Generator guidance and
regression checks now follow the new policy instead of prohibiting repentance.

## Complete decision ledger

Each entry identifies the exact record, decision, context-specific reason and
reader changes. Markers are omitted below for readability; the plan preserves
all unrelated markers and reanchors the terminology note to the discussed term.
A retained decision is deliberate, not an unreviewed omission.

### 1. Prayer of Manasseh 1:8 — POB

Record: [`translation/deuterocanon/prayer_of_manasseh/001/008.yaml`](../translation/deuterocanon/prayer_of_manasseh/001/008.yaml)  
**Decision: revise wording.** The prayer contrasts those for whom repentance was not appointed with the sinful speaker; preserve both negation and contrast without adding a second-person destination to metanoia.

Previous: So you, Lord, God of the righteous, did not appoint reorientation toward you for the righteous—for Abraham and Isaac and Jacob, who did not sin against you—but you appointed reorientation toward you for me, the sinner,

Selected: So you, Lord, God of the righteous, did not appoint repentance for the righteous—for Abraham and Isaac and Jacob, who did not sin against you—but you appointed repentance for me, the sinner,

Reader note: Repentance here means a change of disposition and conduct, not merely feeling sorry or changing an opinion. Sorrow is not excluded. The surrounding passage supplies the direction and consequences; no extra degree of completeness is added.

### 2. Prayer of Manasseh 1:13 — POB

Record: [`translation/deuterocanon/prayer_of_manasseh/001/013.yaml`](../translation/deuterocanon/prayer_of_manasseh/001/013.yaml)  
**Decision: revise wording.** The speaker appeals to the God of those who repent; keep the direct prayer intact without expanding the participle into an added toward-you phrase.

Previous: I beg you, pleading with you: forgive me, Lord, forgive me, and do not destroy me along with my lawless deeds, nor, being angry forever, keep my evils against me, nor condemn me in the lowest parts of the earth; for you, Lord, are the God of those who reorient toward you,

Selected: I beg you, pleading with you: forgive me, Lord, forgive me, and do not destroy me along with my lawless deeds, nor, being angry forever, keep my evils against me, nor condemn me in the lowest parts of the earth; for you, Lord, are the God of those who repent,

Reader note: Repentance here means a change of disposition and conduct, not merely feeling sorry or changing an opinion. Sorrow is not excluded. The surrounding passage supplies the direction and consequences; no extra degree of completeness is added.

### 3. Psalms of Solomon 9:15 — POB

Record: [`translation/deuterocanon/psalms_of_solomon/009/015.yaml`](../translation/deuterocanon/psalms_of_solomon/009/015.yaml)  
**Decision: retain contextual wording.** Retain regret: the recorded metameleia and confession context support contrition; do not imply that all repentance is mere regret.

Selected: You will bless the righteous, and you do not call them to account for the things in which they sinned; and your goodness is toward those who sin with regret.

Reader note: Retain regret: the recorded metameleia and confession context support contrition; do not imply that all repentance is mere regret.

### 4. Psalms of Solomon 16:11 — POB

Record: [`translation/deuterocanon/psalms_of_solomon/016/011.yaml`](../translation/deuterocanon/psalms_of_solomon/016/011.yaml)  
**Decision: retain contextual wording.** Retain turning back: the recorded epistrophe expresses return, not a reason to standardize a different source word as repentance.

Selected: Keep grumbling and faintheartedness in affliction far from me, when I sin, as you discipline me for turning back.

Reader note: Retain turning back: the recorded epistrophe expresses return, not a reason to standardize a different source word as repentance.

### 5. Sirach 17:20 — POB

Record: [`translation/deuterocanon/sirach/017/020.yaml`](../translation/deuterocanon/sirach/017/020.yaml)  
**Decision: revise wording.** Use repent for metanoousin while preserving the distinct return granted to them; avoid duplicating the return expression.

Previous: Yet to those who reorient he granted a return, and he encouraged those who were failing in patience.

Selected: Yet to those who repent he granted a return, and he encouraged those who were failing in patience.

Reader note: Repentance here means a change of disposition and conduct, not merely feeling sorry or changing an opinion. Sorrow is not excluded. The surrounding passage supplies the direction and consequences; no extra degree of completeness is added.

Record: [`translation/deuterocanon/sirach/018/021.yaml`](../translation/deuterocanon/sirach/018/021.yaml)  
**Decision: retain contextual wording.** Retain the visible turning-back expression for epistrophe; humble conduct remains part of its context.

### 7. Wisdom of Solomon 5:3 — POB

Record: [`translation/deuterocanon/wisdom_of_solomon/005/003.yaml`](../translation/deuterocanon/wisdom_of_solomon/005/003.yaml)  
**Decision: retain contextual wording.** Retain reconsidering: the speakers reassess their ridicule in anguish. Do not assert a saving conversion or remove the anguish.

Selected: They will say among themselves, reconsidering, and will groan because of anguish of spirit: “This is the one whom we once held in derision.”

Reader note: Retain reconsidering: the speakers reassess their ridicule in anguish. Do not assert a saving conversion or remove the anguish.

**Decision: revise wording.** Use repentance for the purpose of overlooking sins; keep mercy and purpose without inserting a new toward-you phrase.

Previous: But you have mercy on all, because you are able to do all things, and you overlook the sins of men for reorientation toward you.

Selected: But you have mercy on all, because you are able to do all things, and you overlook the sins of men for repentance.

### 9. Wisdom of Solomon 12:10 — POB

Record: [`translation/deuterocanon/wisdom_of_solomon/012/010.yaml`](../translation/deuterocanon/wisdom_of_solomon/012/010.yaml)  
**Decision: revise wording.** Use room for repentance for topos metanoias; gradual judgment and the opportunity remain distinct from a claim that repentance occurred.

Previous: But in judging them little by little, you were giving room for reorientation toward you, not being unaware that their origin was evil and their wickedness innate, and that their way of thinking would never change forever,

Selected: But in judging them little by little, you were giving room for repentance, not being unaware that their origin was evil and their wickedness innate, and that their way of thinking would never change forever,

Reader note: Repentance here means a change of disposition and conduct, not merely feeling sorry or changing an opinion. Sorrow is not excluded. The surrounding passage supplies the direction and consequences; no extra degree of completeness is added.

### 174. 2 Corinthians 7:9 — POB

Record: [`translation/nt/2_corinthians/007/009.yaml`](../translation/nt/2_corinthians/007/009.yaml)  
**Decision: revise wording.** Restore repentance, retaining grief as what led to it; explain that the resulting change is not identical to the grief.

Previous: Now I rejoice, not because you were grieved, but because you were grieved into a reorientation toward God; for you were grieved according to God, so that you might suffer no loss from us.

Selected: Now I rejoice, not because you were grieved, but because you were grieved into repentance; for you were grieved according to God, so that you might suffer no loss from us.

Reader note: Sorrow leads to or produces repentance here; they are not the same thing. Repentance describes changed disposition and conduct, not merely feeling sorry. The separate language of regret remains distinct.

Selected: For godly sorrow produces repentance leading to salvation without regret, but worldly sorrow produces death.

### 339. 2 Corinthians 7:9 — Simplified POB

Record: [`translation_simplified/nt/2_corinthians/007/009.yaml`](../translation_simplified/nt/2_corinthians/007/009.yaml)  
**Decision: revise wording.** Restore repentance, retaining grief as what led to it; explain that the resulting change is not identical to the grief.

Previous: Now I rejoice—not because you were saddened, but because your sorrow led you to reorientation toward God. You were saddened in a way that was in keeping with God, so that you would not suffer any loss through us.

Selected: Now I rejoice—not because you were saddened, but because your sorrow led you to repentance. You were saddened in a way that was in keeping with God, so that you would not suffer any loss through us.

Reader note: Sorrow leads to or produces repentance here; they are not the same thing. Repentance describes changed disposition and conduct, not merely feeling sorry. The separate language of regret remains distinct.

### 340. 2 Corinthians 7:10 — Simplified POB

Record: [`translation_simplified/nt/2_corinthians/007/010.yaml`](../translation_simplified/nt/2_corinthians/007/010.yaml)

Selected: Sorrow aligned with God produces repentance leading to salvation without regret. But worldly sorrow produces death.

Reader note: Sorrow leads to or produces repentance here; they are not the same thing. Repentance describes changed disposition and conduct, not merely feeling sorry. The separate language of regret remains distinct.
